                                                 PREFACE

         Birth and Deaths are the two most important vital events that define life of an individual. Not
only do they describe the legal existence of an individual, registration of these events is a primary
source of vital data of the population to which they belong. Civil Registration is a continuous,
permanent, compulsory recording of the occurrence and characteristics of vital events, like births,
deaths and still births. These vital statistics are invaluable for planning, monitoring and evaluating
various programmes related to primary health care, family planning, maternal and child health,
education etc. Information collected through the registration process about vital events provides
useful and important statistics.

2. This issue of the Report on `Vital Statistics of India based on the Civil Registration System' for
the year 2024 provides an overview of the working of the Civil Registration System (CRS) in the
country and presents a compilation of data on registered births, deaths, infant deaths and still births
with rural-urban & sex-wise break-up and sex ratio at birth based on Civil Registration Records.
Based on the Annual Reports of the States/UTs, the report for the year 2024 containing key
information of events pertaining to the period 1st January 2024 to 31st December 2024 has been
brought out for the convenience of users.

3. The publication is divided into two parts. Part-I provides a brief review of the Civil Registration
System and contains a summary analysis of data on registered births, deaths, infant deaths and sex
ratio at birth based on registered births at the State level. Part-II consists of the main tables which
provide State and District-wise data on registered births, deaths, infant deaths and still births with
rural-urban & sex-wise break-up.

4. The data source for the report is the statutory Annual Statistical Report prepared by the Chief
Registrars of Births & Deaths of States/UTs. I take this opportunity to express my sincere gratitude
to all the Chief Registrars of Births & Deaths of various States/UTs for their kind cooperation in
providing the requisite information. I am thankful to each one of them for their invaluable and
continuing cooperation.

5. I would like to take this opportunity to mention that the Registration of Births & Deaths Act
(RBD Act), 1969 has been amended for the first time since its inception, through the Registration of
Births & Deaths Act, 1969(Amended in 2023) (No. 20 of 2023) with a view to accommodate changes
and new developments in the society during the last five decades. Various provisions of the Act have
been suitably amended to enhance the convenience of general public in getting the events registered.
I am hopeful that more dynamic implementation of the provisions of the RBD Act 1969(amended in
2023) shall help reduce the time gap and establish this as a reliable source of information on Birth
and Death registration in the country.

6. The report has been prepared by the CRS Section of this office. I would like to place on record
my appreciation for the efforts put in by all the officers and staff working in the CRS Section of the
Office of the Registrar General and the Officers/Staff connected with the Civil Registration System in
the States/UTs.

New Delhi                           Mritunjay Kumar Narayan
July, 2026                          Registrar General of India

Registrar of Births & Deaths of States/UTs or their sub-ordinate offices. The Office of
Registrar General, India (ORGI) has only compiled and collated the data and presented it in

of this Office.

                    Overview of Civil Registration System (CRS)

1. Registration of birth is a right of every child and is the first step towards establishing
         her/his legal identity.

2. It is compulsory to report births and deaths to the Registrar of Births and Deaths under the
         Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969 (as amended in 2023).

3. Births and deaths are registered only at the place of their occurrence.

4. Head of the household is responsible to report births and deaths that occurred in the house
         to the Registrar of Births and Deaths of the concerned area.

5. It is the responsibility of the medical officer in-charge or any person authorised by her/him
         to report the births and deaths that occur in hospitals, maternity or nursing homes, health
         centres or other like institutions to the Registrar of Births and Deaths of the concerned
         area.

6. Provision for use of the Birth Certificate as a single document to prove the date and place
         of birth of a person born on or after the date of commencement of the Registration of Births
         and Deaths (Amendment) Act, 2023, i.e. 1st October, 2023.

7. Births and deaths are reported in the prescribed forms - Form 1 for live birth; Form 2 for
         death and Form 3 for still birth.

8. Midwife or any other medical or health attendant who has attended or was present at the
         time of birth or death are duty bound to notify the concerned Registrar about the
         occurrence of the event.

9. Keeper or the owner of a place set apart for the disposal of the dead bodies or any person
         required by a local authority to be present at such place are required to notify the
         concerned Registrar about the occurrence of the death.

10. The Registrar may either orally or in writing require any person to furnish any information
         within his knowledge in connection with a birth or death in the locality within which such
         person resides and that person shall be bound to comply with such requisition.

11. Births and deaths are to be reported within 21 days of occurrence of the event.

12. The birth and death certificates are issued free of charge by the concerned Registrar for
         events reported within 21 days.

13. Registrar to issue Birth or Death certificate, electronically or otherwise as soon as the
         registration is completed but not later than seven days.

14. The birth/death reported after 21 days of its occurrence but within 30 days can be
         registered on payment of the prescribed late fee.

                                                                            xii
15. The birth/death reported after 30 days but within one year of its occurrence can be
         registered with the written permission of the District Registrar or such other authority and
         on production of self-attested documents and on payment of such fee as prescribed.

16. Births and deaths reported after one year of its occurrence shall be registered only on an
         order of the District Magistrate or Sub-Divisional Magistrate or by an Executive Magistrate
         authorised by the District Magistrate having jurisdiction over the area where birth or death
         has taken place after verifying the correctness of birth and death and on payment of the
         prescribed fee.

17. A birth can be registered without the name of the child.

18. Name of the child can be entered in the birth certificate within 12 months without any fee
         and after one year but within 15 years of registration on payment of prescribed fee and by
         approaching the concerned Registrar.

19. Error(s), if any, in the particulars of birth or death records can be corrected as per the
         prescribed procedure under Section 15 of the Act.

20. Any entry in the birth or death records made fraudulently or improperly, can be cancelled
         by the concerned Registrar.

21. Under Section 17 of the RBD Act, any person can cause a search in the birth or death
         registers for any entry and obtain a certificate of birth or death from such register. Such
         certificates shall be admissible as evidence of birth or death to which the entry relates.

22. All Registrars are required to send a monthly return regarding entries of births and deaths
         in the register kept by them. to the concerned Addl. District Registrar/District Registrar or
         Chief Registrar.

23. It is mandatory for all medical institutions to provide a certificate as to the cause of death
         to the Registrar and a copy of the same to the nearest relative

24. Appointment of Special "Sub-Registrars" in the event of disaster or epidemic for speedy
         registration of deaths and issue of certificates.

25. The birth of an Indian Citizen, occurred outside the country, can also be registered in India
         within sixty days of return of the parents at the place where they intend to settle. After this
         period, it could be registered under delayed registration provisions of the Act.

26. There is no provision to register death of an Indian Citizen that has occurred outside the
         country.

27. Non-reporting of births and deaths, giving false information for inclusion in the register,
         refusing to put signature in the reporting form/register and non-registration of events
         reported to the Registrar is punishable under the Act.

28. Registrars and Sub-Registrars are deemed to be the public servants.

                                                                            xiii
29. Registration of births and deaths made prior to implementation of the RBD Act, 1969
         (amended in 2023) is deemed to have been done under the said Act.

30. On adoption of a child, the name of the adoptive parents and a new name for the child can
         be entered in the birth register.

31. Provision for redressal of grievances of general public aggrieved by any action or order of
         the Registrar or District Registrar.

32. Creation of National and State level databases of registered births and deaths which would
         help in updating other databases resulting in efficient and transparent delivery of public
         services and social benefits.
                                                         

                                                                            xiv
                                      EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

         Civil Registration System (CRS) in India is the unified process of continuous, permanent,
compulsory, and universal recording of vital events (births, deaths, stillbirths) and characteristics
thereof. The Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969 (as amended in 2023) provides for the
compulsory registration of births and deaths. Civil Registration records are the best source of Vital
Statistics. For the individual, records emanating from CRS provides her/his legal identity and
access to the rights of a citizen including entitlements (social benefits provided by the
Government). For the country, the requirement of a complete Civil Registration System is a must
as it has important administrative and statistical uses. The data generated through a complete and
up to date CRS is essential for socio-economic planning and to evaluate the effectiveness of various
social sector programmes. They also serve as the cornerstone of the public health system. The
Annual Report on Vital Statistics of India based on Civil Registration System for 2024 has been
brought out on the basis of reports provided by the Chief Registrar of Births and Deaths of
respective States/Union Territories. The main features of the Annual Report are summarized
below:

Birth Registration:

    · The number of registered births has increased from 252.1 lakhs in 2023 to 254.7 lakhs in 2024.
         (Statement 9, page 40; Chart 1, page 41)

    · Out of the total registered births, the share of male and female is 52.5% and 47.5% respectively.
         (Statement 16, page 50; Chart 6, page 50).

    · The share of births registered in rural and urban is 42.9% and 57.1% respectively. (Statement
         18, page 52; Chart 10 page 53).

    · The share of institutional births to total registered births is 79.4 %. However, this does not
         include information from Sikkim (Chart 14, page 56).

    · The level of registration of births for the year 2024 stood at 99.1%. (Statement 23, page 60;
         Chart 16 page 58).

    · 18 States/UTs have achieved 100% level of registration of births in 2024. (Chart 17, page 59).
    · 13 (population of more than 1 Crore as per Census 2011) out of 19 major States namely West

         Bengal, Uttarakhand, Telangana, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Jharkhand, Bihar, Maharashtra,
         Karnataka, Tamil Nadu, Haryana, Punjab and Gujarat have crossed 90% level of registration of
         births. (Statement 25, page 63)
    · In case of registration of births within the prescribed time period of 21 days, 13 States/UTs
         namely Gujarat, Puducherry, Chandigarh, D & N Haveli and Daman & Diu, Tamil Nadu, A & N

                                                                            xv
         Islands, Kerala, Haryana, Lakshadweep, Goa, Punjab, Himachal Pradesh and West Bengal have
         achieved more than 90% registration of births. (Statement 13, page 45)
    · 2 States i.e. Odisha and Andhra Pradesh have achieved more than 80% to less than or equal to
         90% registration of births within 21 days. (Statement 13, page 45)
    · Further, 13 States/UTs viz. Maharashtra, Chhattisgarh, Assam, Mizoram, Telangana, Tripura,
         Karnataka, Meghalaya, Madhya Pradesh, Uttar Pradesh, Rajasthan, Jharkhand and Delhi are in
         the category of more than 50% to less than or equal to 80% in registering within the stipulated
         time period of 21 days and the remaining 7 States namely Bihar, Jammu & Kashmir,
         Uttarakhand, Ladakh, Manipur, Arunachal Pradesh and Nagaland are under the category of less
         than or equal to 50% in completing the birth registration within the time limit of 21
         days.(Statement 13, page 45). Sikkim has submitted only partial data and therefore not
         considered.
    · Highest Sex Ratio at Birth (SRB) based on Registered events has been reported by Arunachal
         Pradesh (1050), followed by A& N Islands (984), Meghalaya (974), Mizoram (972), and Kerala
         (970). (Statement 26, Page 65; Chart 20, page 66).
    · Lowest SRB has been reported by Nagaland (806) followed by Lakshadweep (865) and
         Jharkhand (890). (Statement 26, Page 65; Chart 20, page 66)

 Still-Birth Registration:

    · The number of registered still births has decreased from 1.01 lakhs in 2023 to 0.81 lakhs in
         2024 (Statement 22, page 57)

    · Share of registered still births in rural and urban area is 31.0% and 69.0% respectively
         (Statement 22, page 57).

Death Registration:

    · The number of registered deaths has increased from 86.6 lakhs in 2023 to 89.4 lakhs in 2024.
         (Statement 9, page 40; Chart 2, page 41)

    · Out of the total registered deaths, the share of male and female is 60.4% and 39.6%
         respectively. (Statement 16, page 50; Chart 7, page 50).

    · The share of deaths registered in rural and urban is 57.2% and 42.8% respectively. (Statement
         18, page 52; Chart 11 page 53).

    · The share of Institutional deaths in total registered deaths is 24.8%. However, this does not
         include information from Sikkim (Chart 15, page 56).

    · The level of registration of death is 99.4% for the year 2024. (Statement 24, page 62; Chart 16
         page 58).

                                                                            xvi
· 21 States/UTs have achieved 100% level of registration of deaths in 2024. (Chart 18, page 61).
· 15 major States out of 19 have crossed 90% level of registration of deaths. These are West

    Bengal, Uttarakhand, Telangana, Tamil Nadu, Punjab, Odisha, Kerala, Karnataka, Haryana,
    Gujarat, Andhra Pradesh, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh and Maharashtra.
    (Statement 25, page 63)
· In case of registration of deaths within the prescribed time period of 21 days, 7 States/UTs
    namely Chandigarh, Punjab, A & N Islands, Puducherry, Haryana, D & N Haveli and Daman &
    Diu and Himachal Pradesh have achieved more than 90% registration of deaths to the total
    deaths registered. (Statement 13, page 45)
· 11 States/UTs i.e. Lakshadweep, Andhra Pradesh, Gujarat, Kerala, Goa, Tamil Nadu, Meghalaya,
    Maharashtra, Chhattisgarh, West Bengal and Telangana have achieved More than 80% to less
    than or equal to 90% registration of deaths within 21 days. (Statement 13, page 45)
· Further, 14 States viz. Tripura, Odisha, Mizoram, Madhya Pradesh, Jammu & Kashmir,
    Rajasthan, Delhi , Uttar Pradesh, Karnataka, Assam, Jharkhand, Uttarakhand, Ladakh and Bihar
    are in the category of More than 50% to less than 80% in registering deaths within the
    stipulated time period of 21 days and remaining 3 states namely Manipur, Nagaland, and
    Arunachal Pradesh are under the category of less than or equal to 50% in completing the death
    registration within the time limit of 21 days. (Statement 13, page 45)
· In the case of registration of infant deaths, the share of the urban area is 84.2% compared to
    rural with only 15.8%. (Statement 20, page 56).

                                                     

                                                                      xvii
                                      GLOSSARY OF TERMS

Birth: Live birth or still birth.
Live birth: Complete expulsion or extraction from its mother of a product of conception, irrespective of the
duration of pregnancy, which, after such expulsion or extraction, breathes or shows any other evidence of
life, and each product of such birth is considered live-born.
Still Birth: Foetal death where a product of conception has attained at least the prescribed period of
gestation.
Death: Permanent disappearance of all evidence of the life at any time after live birth has taken place.
Infant Death: Death of a child less than one year old.
Level of Registration: The level of registration, defined as the percentage of registered births/deaths to
the births/deaths estimated through SRS, determines the performance level of a State / Union territory
with regard to functioning of Civil Registration System.

                                                                           
                             () =        × 100

                                    =  -   ×  

Mid-year Population: Population at the mid-point of the year, i.e., 1st July of the year provided by
population projections based on 2011. (Source: Population Projections for India and States 2011­2036,
Report of the Technical Group on Population Projections constituted by National Commission on
Population, July. 2020)

of Registered Birth

                                                 2
                                                    CHAPTER ­ I

                          CIVIL REGISTRATION SYSTEM - AN INTRODUCTION

1.1 The requirement for a complete and up to date Vital Statistical System to yield reliable
data on vital events hardly needs any emphasis. These data are essential for socio-economic
planning and development and also to evaluate the effective implementation of various public
schemes and programs. The main sources of data on vital events in India are Civil Registration
System (CRS), Sample Registration System (SRS) and Population Census. Though the Population
Census is the main source of information on population and its characteristics; however, being
a decennial exercise, it does not provide the measure of changes in population from year to year.
The measures of fertility and mortality derived from Census are centered on the midpoint of the
decade and as such do not provide yearly change in the population. Of the other two sources of
vital statistics for getting the vital rates on a regular basis, the SRS has been designed to provide
reliable estimates at National and State level. Robust estimates of vital rates at District level on
an annual basis through sample survey are difficult to obtain on account of prohibitively large
sample size and variety of resultant factors such as controlling of non-sampling errors etc. CRS
is thus the only source for providing vital rates at district level. Rather, a complete CRS can
provide these rates at lower administrative levels as well.

1.2 The Civil Registration System (CRS) may be defined as a unified process of continuous,
permanent, compulsory and universal recording of the vital events and characteristics thereof,
as per legal requirements in the country. In India, the Civil Registration System covers
registration of births and deaths. Registration of marriages and divorce are not covered under
the CRS in India. Civil Registration System provides the best source of information on the vital
rates at all levels. Ensuring cent percent coverage of vital events by registration machinery can
help in getting the critical vital statistics at all administrative levels, important for planning and
research. Therefore, emphasis should also be given to the proper monitoring of the activities
under the system. Civil Registration System is considered to be complete if it covers 100 percent
births and deaths taking place within a country or area and birth and death certificates are issued
to all registered events.

1.3 The registration records are primarily useful for their value as legal documents and
secondarily as the source of vital statistics. Civil registration as a source of vital statistics is
relatively less expensive, as the statistics generated from the registration records are the by-
products of an essential administrative process. The registration records can also generate
current and continuous statistics, which can be more accurate than any other method of data
collection apart from being a continuous source of such data. However, the accuracy of the vital
statistics obtained through the registration system depends much on the completeness and
promptness with which events are registered and the correctness of the information in the
registration records. The improvement in the system of registration of births and deaths is
critically dependent on the importance given by the respective States and District
administrations to this work and regular monitoring by them. Besides, the level of awareness

                                                                            3
among the general public about the utility of birth or death certificate is also equally and crucially
important.

1.4 Vital statistics obtained through registration records at different administrative levels
enable estimation of the size, demographic structure and geographical distribution of the
population except for migration. Vital statistics also enables the projections for coming years on
the basis of probable trends of fertility and mortality as derived from these vital statistics and
their interactions with social, economic and other demographic factors. The vital statistics data
generated through an effective Civil Registration System is of immense use for formulation of
various development and welfare programmes/ schemes and increases their effectiveness at
State and District level. The Civil Registration data acquires paramount importance in the wake
of 73rd and 74th amendments to the constitution of India, as it can provide data at local level for
micro level planning, monitoring and evaluation of schemes. At national level, the vital statistics
data generated through Civil Registration is also quite useful for medical research and in the
study of sex ratio, mortality rates and also in the study of causes of deaths.

Registration of births and deaths in India

1.5 In India, the registration of births and deaths is mandatory under provisions of the
Registration of Births and Deaths (RBD) Act, 1969(18 of 1969). The Act is administered by the
Office of the Registrar General of India (ORGI) and implemented by States/UTs. The Chief
Registrar is the Chief executive authority in the State for carrying into execution the provisions
of the Act and the rules and orders made thereunder.

1.6 Provision for registration of births and deaths and the mechanism thereof had been in
existence in India for more than a century. Earlier, the work of registration of birth and death
was carried out under various laws and by-laws existing at that time in different parts of the
country. The need for improvement in the vital statistics system was felt for long and, therefore,
after detailed deliberations at various levels, the Registration of Births & Deaths Act, 1969 was
enacted providing compulsory registration of births and deaths in India. Since then, this Act has
been enforced uniformly across all the States and Union Territories (UTs). This Act provides for
mandatory registration of births and deaths and publication of statistical reports annually based
on the civil registration data. The enactment of this Act has provided a common framework for
putting in place an effective system of registration of births and deaths in the country.

1.7 In order to simplify and speed up the process of registration and to bring in uniformity in
birth and death registration formats, the registration system was revamped in the year 2000.
Under the revamped system, the procedure for registration of births and deaths was also
modified to some extent and the forms were redesigned. The revamped system became
operational in 2000 in all States/Union Territories except Assam, Arunachal Pradesh, Gujarat,
Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Jharkhand, Odisha, Punjab, Uttarakhand, and Uttar Pradesh. It was
implemented in Assam, Arunachal Pradesh, and Odisha in 2001, Haryana in 2002, Himachal
Pradesh, Uttarakhand, and Uttar Pradesh in 2003, Gujarat and Punjab in 2004, and Jharkhand in
2009.

                                                                            4
1.8 The Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969 has been amended through the
Registration of Births and Deaths (Amendment) Act, 2023 (No. 20 of 2023) and published in the
Gazette of India on 11th August, 2023. The provisions of the amended Act have come into force
with effect from 1st day of October, 2023 (Gazette of India, No. 3896 dated 13th September,
2023). The highlights of amendment may be seen in the following box:

               Major Highlights of Registration of Births and Deaths
                                (Amendment) Act, 2023

     (i) Insertion of provisions for digital registration and electronic delivery of certificate of births and
            deaths for the benefit of public at large;

     (ii) Creation of National and State level databases of registered births and deaths which would help in
            updating other databases resulting in efficient and transparent delivery of public services and social
            benefits;

     (iii) Provision for use of the Birth Certificate as a single document to prove the date and place of birth of
            a person born on or after the date of commencement of the Registration of Births and Deaths
            (Amendment) Act, 2023, i.e. 1st October, 2023, for admission to an educational institution, issuance
            of a driving license, preparation of a voter list, registration of a marriage, appointment to a post in
            Central Government or State Government or a local body or public sector undertaking or in any
            statutory or autonomous body under the Central Government or State Government, issuance of a
            passport, issuance of an Aadhaar number and any other purpose as may be determined by the
            Central Government in order to enhance the public convenience and to avoid multiplicity of
            documents to prove date and place of birth in the country;

     (iv) Changing the ordering authority from Magistrate of the first class or Presidency Magistrate to
            District Magistrate or Sub-Divisional Magistrate or an Executive Magistrate authorized by the
            District Magistrate in the case of delayed information of any birth or death to the Registrar after one
            year of its occurrence and submission of self-attested document instead of an affidavit made before
            a Notary Public in the case of delayed information of any birth or death to the Registrar after thirty
            days but within one year of its occurrence;

     (v) Facilitate registration process of adopted, orphan, abandoned, surrendered, surrogate child and
            child to a single parent or unwed mother;

     (vi) It is mandatory for all medical institutions to provide a certificate as to the cause of death to the
            Registrar and a copy of the same to the nearest relative;

     (vii) Appointment of Special "Sub-Registrars" in the event of disaster or epidemic for speedy registration
            of deaths and issue of certificates;

     (viii) Collection of Aadhaar numbers of parents and informant, if available, in case of birth registration;
     (ix) Provision for redressal of grievances of general public aggrieved by any action or order of the

            Registrar or District Registrar; and
     (x) Enhance the penalties provided in the Act.

                                                                            5
Statistical Reports
1.9 System of registration of births and deaths is constantly generating statistical
information at the local level. The information contained in the reporting forms is consolidated
by the Registrar for onward transmission to the Chief Registrar of Births & Deaths or to any
officer specified in the concerned State/UT. The Chief Registrar of the States/UTs, who are the
chief executive authorities for the activities on registration of Births and Deaths in the State/UT,
are mandated to submit to the State Government Annual Report on the Working of the Act in the
State/UT along with the Statistical Report referred under section 19(2) on registered births and
deaths for each year immediately following the year to which the report relates to. The Chief
Registrars are also required to send a copy of these Reports to the Registrar General of India,
Govt. of India.
1.10 Based on the Annual Reports prepared by the States/UTs, the Office of the Registrar
General of India, brings out an annual comprehensive report entitled "Vital Statistics of India
based on Civil Registration System". These reports have been brought out since 1958 (Before
the enactment of the Act, only statistical reports were brought out). However, some of the States
are not bringing out their Annual Statistical Reports in time, and this in turn, delays the
publication of the corresponding national report. It has also been observed that some States also
do not include all the prescribed tables in their reports thereby creating a limitation while
bringing out the national report. However, notwithstanding all these constraints, this report
provides vital indicators including the District-wise registered births and deaths along with
infant deaths with the sex-wise and rural-urban break-up of registered events for the year 2024.

                                                        

                                                                            6
                                         CHAPTER-II

                        ANNUAL REPORT FOR 2024 ON THE WORKING OF
               THE REGISTRATION OF BIRTHS AND DEATHS (RBD) ACT, 1969

                                            (AMENDED IN 2023)

2.1. Annual report on the working of the Registration of Births and Deaths (RBD)

Act, 1969(Amended in 2023): Under the provision of Section 4 (4) of the Act, the Chief

Registrars are required to submit an Annual Report on the working of the RBD Act to the
respective State Governments once approved, a copy of the same is sent to the Office of
Registrar General of India (ORGI). The ORGI compiles the reports received from States/UTs
and release the working of RBD Act report at National level. The aforesaid Annual Report for
the year 2024 has been received from all the States/UTs.
2.2. Registration Machinery: Section 3 to 7 of the Registration of Births and Deaths
(RBD)Act, 1969(amended in 2023) provides for the appointment of Civil Registration System
functionaries at all administrative and Jurisdictional levels viz.: -

(i)  National level:- Registrar General of India; (Section 3 of the RBD Act)

(ii) State level:- Chief Registrar; (Section 4 of the Act)

(iii) District level: - District Registrar/Additional District Registrar (Section 6 of the Act )

(iv) Local area level: - Registrar for a village, Panchayat, municipality, municipal

     Corporation, or other local bodies; (Section 7(1) of the Act), and;

(v) Sub-Registrar for specified area within the area under the jurisdiction of a Registrar

     (Section 7 (5) of the Act).

           The Registrar General of India (RGI) at Central level takes steps to coordinate and
unify the activities of Chief Registrars of all States/Union Territories and issues general
directions regarding registration of births and deaths under section 3(3) of the Act. The work
relating to Civil Registration in the office of the Registrar General of India is handled by the
Vital Statistics Division.

           The Chief Registrar, appointed by the State Government, is the chief executive
authority in the respective States/ UTs for implementing the provisions of RBD Act and the
Rules and orders made thereunder. The State Government also appoints District Registrar of
births and deaths for each revenue district. The actual work of registration of births and

                                                                     7
deaths in the field is carried out by the local Registrars, who are also appointed by the State
Government

           There is a wide variation in the registration machinery across the country, details of
which are given below: -

  The Chief Registrars of births and deaths in 17 States (Andhra Pradesh, Assam,

Gujarat, Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Maharashtra, Manipur, Meghalaya, Odisha, Punjab,

Sikkim, Tamil Nadu, Telangana, Tripura, Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand and West Bengal) and 4

Union Territories (A& N Islands, Chandigarh, Ladakh and Lakshadweep) are from the Health

Department, in 10 States (Arunachal Pradesh, Bihar, Chhattisgarh, Goa, Jharkhand, Karnataka,

Madhya Pradesh, Mizoram, Nagaland and Rajasthan) and 2 Union Territories (Delhi and

Jammu & Kashmir) are from Planning, Economics & Statistics Department, in 1 State (Kerala)

from Local Self Government Department, in UT of Dadra & Nagar Haveli & Daman & Diu,

Finance Secretary/Secretary (Planning & Statistics) and in remaining 1 UT (Puducherry) it is

with Local Administration Department.

  The district level authorities in the States/UTs such as District Collectors/ District

Magistrate, Additional Collector, Chief Medical Officers/ District Medical and Health, Civil

Surgeon, District Statistical/ Planning Officers, Deputy Commissioner, Deputy Director

(Panchayat) and Dy. Director (local administration) are appointed as District Registrar.

  In rural areas, the registration work is entrusted to the officials of Panchayat

Department, Health Department, Revenue Department and Education Department. While in

urban areas, the Health Officer of Municipal Corporation / Municipalities etc. or in absence of

such a post, the Executive Officer of the town administration functions as the Registrar of

                                       8
Births and Deaths (State and UT wise details of registration functionaries are attached at
Annexure-A).

2.3 Registration of Births and Deaths occurring to Indian Citizens abroad: Under the
provisions of the Citizenship Act, 1955, events relating to the births and deaths of Indian
citizens occurring abroad are registered by the respective Indian Missions and Consulates.
Further, under Rule 5 of the Citizens (Registration at Indian Consulates) Rules, 1956, Indian
Consulates are statutorily required to send annual returns of such births and deaths to the
office of the Registrar General of India (RGI).

           In this regard, Section 20(1) of the Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969, as
amended in 2023, provides a special provision for the registration of births and deaths of
Indian citizens outside India. The provision States that the Registrar General of India shall,
subject to such rules as may be made by the Central Government in this behalf, cause to be
registered information as to births and deaths of citizens of India outside India received by him
under the rules relating to the registration of such citizens at Indian Consulates made under the
Citizenship Act, 1955 (57 of 1955), and every such registration shall also be deemed to have been
duly made under the RBD Act(amended in 2023).

           It has been observed that requisite returns are not furnished by all Indian
Missions/Consulates; such returns for the year 2024 have been received from 182 missions
located in 140 countries, covering 66413 births and 11384 deaths. As can be seen from the
Statement 3 below, out of the total registered births at the various missions during 2024
Mission/Consulate in Dubai, UAE registered 13927 i.e. about 21.0 per cent of the total births
registered abroad, followed by Mission/Consulate in London which is at 11.5 per cent. In case
of Deaths, Mission/Consulate in Dubai, UAE registered 2020 i.e. 17.7 per cent of the total
deaths registered abroad followed by Mission/Consulate in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia which is at
11.8 per cent.

2.4  Inter Departmental Coordination Committees (IDCC): In order to resolve the

operational problems and for proper implementation of the provisions of the RBD Act and

corresponding State Rules, coordination among the implementing departments is essentially

required. On the other hand, the work of registration of births and deaths in most of the

States/UTs is being done through the officials drawn from more than one department of the

State Government/Union Territory Administration. The need for coordination between the

stake holder departments is a pre-condition for effective functioning of the system in each of

the State. In view of above, the State level Inter-Departmental Coordination Committees

(IDCCs) have been constituted in States and Union Territories. In most of the States / UTs,

IDCC is chaired by the Principal Secretary/Secretary of the Department to which the Chief

Registrar belongs.

     In order to formulate the strategies for further improvements in Civil Registration

System and effective implementation of the provisions of the RBD Act in the respective State,

it is desired to convene the meeting of such committee at least once in a year. However, it has

                               10
been observed that despite persuasion of this office from time to time, such meetings have not
been convened regularly by most of the States/ UTs.

        During 2024, IDCC meetings were held in only 21 States/UTs namely Arunachal
Pradesh, Bihar, Chhattisgarh, Gujarat, Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Karnataka, Kerala, Madhya
Pradesh, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Nagaland, Odisha, Punjab, Rajasthan, Tripura, Uttar Pradesh,
Uttarakhand, West Bengal, Chandigarh, and Puducherry.

          Such Coordination Committees have also been set up at District and below district

level (i.e. at Mandal and Taluka level) in many States. However, the information on convening

the regular meeting of District Level Coordination Committees (DLCCs) and below district

level committee meetings has not been received from all the States/UTs. The following

information regarding district level and below district level coordination committee meetings

during 2024 has been provided by the Chief Registrars of State/UT in their respective annual

report:-

          Andhra Pradesh: During the year 2024, no IDCC meeting was held. However, 2

meetings were held at Chief Secretary level on CRS during the year with all other line

department officials. It gave a boost to CRS Programme in the state.

          Arunachal Pradesh: During the year 2024, one IDCC Meeting was conducted by the

Chief Registrar of Births & Deaths in the State Headquarter, Itanagar, while no DLCC meeting

was conducted during the period.

          Assam: Though, No IDCC meeting was held during the year 2024, but similar

2024 in the State.

       Karnataka: Registration of birth and death events is being carried out online in the

State through uniform web-based application namely e-JanMa developed by State NIC since

2018.

       Kerala:

· Civil Registration has been computerized in all the registration units by using the softwares

  developed by Information Kerala Mission. `Sevana', the software application developed by

  IKM for the registration of births and deaths was launched in the year 2005 in all the 5

  Municipal Corporations of the State and subsequently extended to all the Municipalities,

  Grama Panchayats and Cantonment Board. The computerized registration system was fully

  implemented in all the Registration Units by the year 2009. Later, an exclusive web site

  (cr.lsgkerala.gov.in) was launched for civil registration, where the certificates of

  registrations in the Sevana local application were made available within 24 hours of

  registration of the event and could be downloaded by the public

·      Under the Hospital Kiosk Project, online registration of birth and death events

occurring in hospitals has been implemented in 6 Corporations, 70 Municipalities and 243

Gram Panchayats up to the end of 2024.

·      Under this Project, the report of births and deaths are sent to the Registration Unit as

electronic data through telephone line from the kiosks, which are functioning in the hospital.

The electronic report received at the Registration Unit is registered after verification and the

certificate under Section 12 is issued through the hospital. Efforts are made to issue the birth

certificate before the mother and baby leaves the hospital.

·      K-SMART, a new web application was implemented in all Urban Local Self

Governments of the State with effect from 1st April, 2024. The system was implemented in

one Grama Panchayat also as a pilot for the implementation in all rural Local Governments.

The civil registration records of Sevana software were transferred to K-SMART and the

reporting of the events are being done through the login of the individuals and persons

responsible for the non-domestic events.

                                          16
  Madhya Pradesh: Online registration of births and deaths has been initiated from

01-01-2015 in the State of Madhya Pradesh using ORGI uniform CRS portal. During 2024,

1996172 and 573608 births and deaths were registered respectively.

  Maharashtra: In the urban and rural areas of the State, the ORGI uniform CRS portal

is being used for registration of birth and death events from 2016 onwards.

  Manipur: Since 15th May 2024, all registration Units are using Online CRSORGI Portal

for registration in the State.

  Meghalaya: Online registration process was implemented in 46 Registration Units.

Computerization of old Civil Registration Records has not been possible due to lack of fund.

  Mizoram: Digitization of all the Civil Registration Records in Mizoram was completed

during 2019. These digitized records contain some minor errors and the same are being

rectified. Online Registration is not yet done at the Registration Unit level. However, online

submission to ORGI's CRS portal of all the reports of live Births, still births and deaths are done

at the respective ADR Offices. Moreover, all the records are also kept in digitized form in the

State specific software 'CRS Mizoram'.

  Nagaland: Births & Deaths data is compiled through computerization in every

district headquarter. Online registration of births and deaths through CRS ORGI portal was

launched in the state on 19th April 2022. Currently the Registration units in the village/block

level are functioning manually only but efforts are being made through training for

digitization of CRS in the rural areas. Online registration at district level not functional due to

connectivity issues. The State is taking concrete steps to upgrade the ICT capability of the

district headquarters for implementation of the ORGI uniform CRS portal for online

registration.

  Odisha: Online registration of births and deaths in 116 urban registration units and

all 314 Rural registration units started from 01.01.2021 in OBDRS portal, developed by the

State, and is continuing till date.

  Punjab: The e-Sewa application was introduced by the Department of Governance

Reforms (DoGR), Punjab in July, 2019 after revamping the earlier e-District application. The

services of issuance of birth and death certificate, addition of name of child, correction in

record, delayed registration of birth and death and inclusion of adoptive parents' names in

birth certificate under Adoption Services have been included in the e-Sewa application and

541 Sewa Kendra's were established in the State. All the birth and death certificates are now

being issued electronically through eSewa portal. If a citizen applies for a birth or death

certificate and the relevant registration record is still maintained in manual form then the

                                        17
related entry is digitized through eSewa application and then a computer-generated

certificate is issued.

         Rajasthan: The online registration of births and deaths through the web portal

`Pehchan' is being done since 01.01.2014 in the State. 100 percent computerization and online

registration is going on in the State.

         Sikkim: In the State, out of total 33 registration units, 19 were working online since

2019 and since March 2023, all 14 remaining registrations units started functioning online on

ORGI uniform CRS software (www.crsorgi.gov.in). Registration of births and deaths record in

physical form for the period 2015 to Dec, 2022 around 40,000 data records digitization

process is going on with the fund provided by the state government

         Tamil Nadu: The common CRS software (www.crstn.org) is implemented uniformly

throughout the State from 2018 onwards for registration of birth and death and to

issue/download the birth and death certificates. In order to have the comprehensive CRS

database at the state level, data entry of old records pertaining to the period 2013 to 2017

have been completed and the State is under process to bring the old records into live in TNCRS

portal.

         Telangana: Civil registration records are fully digitalized in the State.

         Tripura: The work of online registration of births & deaths and reporting of each

event has been started through ORGI uniform CRS software all over the State from 8th April

2016 onwards.

         Uttarakhand: The online registration of births & deaths is done in complete State

through ORGI uniform CRS software (www.dc.crsorgi.gov.in). Due to lack of fully dedicated

manpower for Birth and Death Registration program in the districts computerization of old

registration record is affected.

         Uttar Pradesh: Birth and death registration in the state is done using online ORGI

Uniform Registration of Births (CRS) portal, and computerized birth and death certificates are

issued by all registration units. Tablets have been provided to health units to implement the

Aadhaar-based birth registration process for newborn birth registration. Technical issues in

2024 hindered the implementation of the project. Additionally, birth and death registration

data were linked to various beneficiary schemes, such as the Shramik Kalyan Yojana,

Mukhyamantri Kanya Sumangala Yojana, and Parivar Labh Yojana, through API-based

authentication. The Mantra newborn tracking application used in government hospitals was

integrated with the CRS portal, enabling birth certificates to be provided to mothers before

discharge.

                                        18
         West Bengal: The State's own portal Janma Mrityu Tathya (JMT) has been used since

5th May, 2022 and the use of ORGI Portal has been discontinued. CRS Portal & JMT Portal have

been integrated and almost all data registered in CRS Portal are available as old legacy data in

JMT Portal. During the year, "1415351" births and "687348" deaths were registered using the

State's own Software.

         Andaman & Nicobar Islands: The ORGI uniform CRS online software

(www.crsorgi.gov.in) has been implemented in all the registration units w.e.f. 01-01-2016 and

all the old birth and death records have been uploaded in CRS portal w.e.f. Aug 1982.

         Chandigarh: Registration units of UT are working on the CRS portal since October

2015.

         Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu: Registration of birth and deaths in all the

registration centers in Rural as well as Urban area have been computerized and online

registration is being carried out.

         Delhi: All local bodies i.e. New Delhi Municipal Council (NDMC), Municipal

Corporation of Delhi (MCD) and Delhi Cantonment Board (DCB)have computerized the birth

& deaths registration system. All hospital are reporting the events online to the concerned

registrar (Births & Deaths). NIC has developed new software for Birth and Death registration

process, addition of name of child, correction in birth/ death records in Municipal Corporation

of Delhi. This has enabled a complete online system to facilitate citizen to apply Birth and

death registration/certificates from their home. Birth/death certificates can also be

downloaded by the Citizens from the websites of concerned local bodies. In NDMC & DCB,

almost all hospital are reporting the vital events online. Soft copy of the CRS data is supplied

along with MCCD data to chief Registrar's office by the local bodies for preparation of annual

report.

         Jammu & Kashmir: Registration of Births and Deaths is being carried out through

the CRS portal developed by the Office of the Registrar General of India (ORGI) in almost all

registration units across Jammu & Kashmir, except in the Cantonment Boards of Satwari

(Jammu) and Badami Bagh (Srinagar). Computerization of old manual registration records

shall be undertaken as and when the same is handed over by the erstwhile Registrars (SHOs)

in respect of Rural areas.

         Ladakh: The online Registration system CRS started in UT ­ Ladakh from December,

2022 onwards.

         Lakshadweep: Birth and death registration was computerized starting in 2015, and

currently, all old data prior to 2014 has been fully digitalized. Additionally, the online receipt

                                    19
of payment for the registration of births and deaths has been introduced through the CRSORGI

portal, with approval from the competent authority. The agreement between ORGI, NSDL, and

the UTLA was signed, and the online payment system has been operational since 22nd March

2024. Birth and death certificates are now being provided in digital format.

  Puducherry: All local bodies are issuing computerized birth and death certificates

for the event registered under the Act. In order to cater the needs of the public who requires

birth and death certificates of their ancestors, computerization of birth and death record has

been taken up for the period prior to 1979 in Puducherry and Oulgaret Municipalities. The

computerized birth records from 1930 and death records from 1954 are available in

Puducherry Municipality. In Oulgaret Municipality birth records from 1920 and death records

from 1979 are available.

2.6. Penalties: There is a provision for imposing penalties on defaulting institutions,

and the individuals for non-reporting and for providing false information and also on

Registrars for negligence or refusal to register any birth or death without any reasonable

cause under section 23 of the RBD Act, 1969. Despite the aforesaid provision, most of the State

Governments are not imposing penalties on the defaulters. The details of imposing penalties

and prosecutions were not provided by majority of States. However, a few States have

initiated actions in this regard, the details of which are given below: -

  Andhra Pradesh: during the year 2024, the District Registrars issued show-cause

notices to the Registrars, where Births and Deaths events were registered without proper field

verification.

  Arunachal Pradesh: No penalties were imposed under section 23 of the RBD

Act, 1969 during 2024 on defaulting institutions, Registrars, and individuals for non-

registration /non-reporting and for negligence to register any birth/death.

  Assam: Two prosecutions were done of CRS functionaries during the year 2024, Also,

some of the officials were called for explanation for irregular practices and reminded not to

delay the process/undue practice.

  Gujarat: During the year 2024, no notices to Taluka/Municipalities have been issued

to provide information of Births & Deaths in time. No prosecutions were reported under

section 23 and 24. Penalization was avoided and improvement was attempted through

educative and persuasive measures.

  Haryana: All medical institutions in the state have been registered in the CRS portal

and are reporting the events online in time. There was no such institution in knowledge which

                                    20
did not reporting births / deaths events in CRS portal. Also, some of the birth and death

registration services have been covered under Haryana Right to Service Act, 2014. These

services such as application for issuance of Birth / Death / Non-Availability Certificate (NAC),

Application for Correction in Birth or Death Record, Application for Inclusion of Child Name

in Birth Record and Application for Delayed Birth/Death Registration are covered under the

Haryana RTS Act. Time frame for each and every service has been fixed under the RTS Act and

regular monitoring is being done at senior level. Hence no delay in action at the level of

Registrar was observed. Therefore, no penalty was imposed on institution during year 2024.

  Karnataka: No prosecution was launched during the year. Penalization was avoided

and improvement was attempted through educative, publicity/propaganda and persuasive

measures.

  Kerala: During 2024, the maximum numbers of prosecutions (10) were from

Kozhikkode Panchayat followed by Mattanoor Municipality (5). The maximum number of

offences 4 each were compounded in Palakkad and Kannur Panchayat. State wise details of

Penalties imposed are given in Statement 5.

                                                      

  Punjab: Penalty on two persons was imposed U/s 23 of the RBD Act during the year

2024 for violation of the provisions of RBD Act.

  Rajasthan: Penal action taken against institutions, registrars and individuals in

rural/urban areas under Section 23 of RBD Act, 1969 (amended in 2023) for non-

registration/non-reporting and negligence. In the year 2024, a total of Rs 765642 was

recovered by imposing penalties in total 2550 cases.

  Tamil Nadu: During the year 2024, Rs 9350 was collected through 15 Penalties

imposed.

  Uttarakhand: No incident of prosecution and compounding of offences has taken

place in the State. Improvement has been made by giving adequate publicity to educate and

sensitize general public through appeal to stay away from wrong doing and legal tangle.

  Uttar Pradesh: Instructions have been issued from time to time to the district

officers to take action as per the provisions contained in the Act.

  Chandigarh: In the year 2024, Rs.19620 was collected by imposing the penalties

were imposed under section 23 of the RBD Act, 1969 on individuals for negligence to report

birth or death.

2.7. Inspection of Registration units: Section 18 of the RBD Act, 1969 provides for
inspection of registration offices and records in order to improve the registration system in
qualitative as well as quantitative terms. The inspections are required to be done by the office
of the Chief Registrar and District Registrars in each State/UT. Such inspections could have
been more meaningful, if organized and conducted frequently within a regular time frame.
However, as per the information provided by the States, the inspections of registration centers
were observed to be irregular and extremely deficient in most of the States/UTs. The actual
information in respect of number of units inspected is not provided by majority of the States
/UTs, the details of information regarding inspection provided by some of the States/ UTs
during the year are given below:

  Chhattisgarh: 462 Registration Units covering rural/urban/institutional units were

  Goa: In order to improve the quality and functioning of the Births and Deaths

Registration System in the State, regular inspections are conducted by the Office of the Chief

Registrar of Births and Deaths for checking the physical as well as computerized records

maintained therein.

  Gujarat: During the year 2024, 6541 Inspections were carried out in the State.

officials of Revenue Department. 1815 inspections of the registration units were done by the

District Statistical Officers of the Directorate and Officers/Officials of the Revenue Department

for the year 2024.

  Kerala: In all units of Kerala, the Internal Vigilance Officer under the concerned Joint

Director conducts quarterly inspections. The District Registrar forward the report with

specific remarks to the Chief Registrar. Efforts are made to issue instructions to rectify the

defects pointed out in the inspection reports.

Jiribaum District and Imphal West District.

Inspections also included cross-verification of accident deaths, suicides, and homicides

reported by police departments to ensure accurate data entry. Deputy Directors(Training &

2.8. Training of Civil Registration Functionaries- Civil Registration functionaries

involved in registration of births and deaths at various levels in most of the States/UTs belong

to different departments. Almost all of these functionaries perform registration work as an

additional work along with other work assigned to them by their respective department. In

addition, due to frequent transfer of registration functionaries, there is a need to impart

training at regular intervals on RBD Act, 1969 (Amended in 2023) and registration rules,

procedures and practices being followed in the State/UT. As per the information furnished by

the States, the details of training programs organized in various States during the year 2024

are given below:

       Andhra Pradesh: Virtual trainings were conducted for newly recruited/ promoted /

transferred registrars and assisting staff in the entire state.

       Arunachal Pradesh: CRS /MCCD Training Programme was organized at Directorate

of Economics & Statistics (DES) - HQ for officials during 2024.

                               26
  Bihar: 30 Training programmes for officers and officials were organized at district

level. Also, 272 trainings were conducted at block levels during the year 2024 for capacity

building.

  Chhattisgarh: 71 training programmes on provisions of RBD Act and State rules

framed thereunder were organized for Civil Registration functionaries during the year 2024.

  Gujarat: During the year 2024, training was conducted for District registrars, Births

& Deaths. Also, the trainings on e-olakh Application have been given to Registrar, Birth and

Death through SATCOM as well as Webex . This training was specifically designed for key

officials, such as District Registrar (Chief District Health Officer), Medical Officer of Health

(Municipal Corporation), Chief Officer (Municipality), Talati (Gram Panchayat) etc.

  Haryana: During the year 2024, training on new CRS portal was organized at State

HQ with the Support of ORGI. Additional District Registrar (Births &Deaths) of districts along

with other officials related to birth & death registration work have attended this training. They

were informed about the new features in revamped CRS portal.

  Himachal Pradesh:, 30 Registrars were trained in the District of Solan during the

year 2024.

  Jharkhand: 24 District level and 264 block level training programmes were

organized during the year 2024. These programmes were attended by 1443 and 10570

participants respectively at District and block level.

  Karnataka: 95 trainings were conducted during 2024. District Statistical Officers

conducted training programs in all the Taluks and in Bruhath Bangalore Mahanagara Palike

to improve the Civil Registration System. Detailed procedures of RBD Act and State Rules,

clarifications and discrepancies in filling up of reporting forms and use of e-JanMa software

were also discussed during training.

  Kerala: CRS training to all local registrars was conducted under the leadership of

Kerala Institute of Local Administration using the funds of the state government during the

year 2024. The staff of the hospital kiosks have been trained online with the help of software

under the guidance of Chief Registrar and Additional Chief Registrar to ensure accuracy of

information in birth and death registrations.

  Madhya Pradesh: Training Programmes are conducted regularly in the State to

bring the quantitative and qualitative improvements. During the year 2024, 369 training

programmes were conducted in which 25948 civil registration functionaries were trained.

                                      27
        Manipur: The civil registration functionaries organized a comprehensive training

initiatives encompassing seven programs: 4 online registration portal and 3 MCCD programs

during the year 2024.

        Meghalaya: During the year 2024, With IPA funding, Hands-On Training was

conducted on online registration of births and deaths for 25 participants.

        Mizoram: During the year 2024, annual training of RBD, Act and Rules was convened

at all 11 district headquarters and at the same time general guidelines and registration

procedure including frequently asked questions (FAQ) in Mizo (local) dialect were issued by

office of the Additional Chief Registrar and the same was distributed to all Registration Units.

        Nagaland: A one day Training of Trainers on new CRS Portal was conducted in the

DES with officials from ORGI as resource persons to train the district staff as resource persons

for imparting training to Registrars of Births & Deaths in their respective districts. DES also

conducted training on online Registration of Births & Deaths for the Registrars of Births &

Deaths in all districts during 2024 with the aim to digitize the issuance of births and deaths

certificates through CRS ORGI Portal in all urban and rural areas of the State.

        Odisha: 4 Training programmes were conducted during the year 2024.

        Punjab: The training of new officials and re-orientation courses for registration

functionaries are arranged at State headquarters to keep the functionaries well versed with

the latest clarifications and provisions of the Act. The participants are also attending various

courses at the Regional Health Statistics Training Centre SAS Nagar and Health and Family

Welfare Training Centre, Amritsar. The State Institute of Health and Family Welfare Training,

Mohali has also imparting trainings on the subject of registration of births and deaths. Apart

from this, Department of Governance Reforms is also providing training to all the registration

functionaries involved in the process of various birth and death related services under E-Sewa

regarding usage of this application. Department also conducted training sessions for

registration functionaries on the use of Aadhaar based birth or death registration on a trial

basis.

        Rajasthan: 1 State level, 24 District level and 100 block level training programmes

were organized in the State for Civil Registration functionaries during the year 2024. In all,

125 Training programmes were conducted in which 6077 participants were trained.

        Tamil Nadu: Frequent orientation sessions were conducted at district and state

levels, including updates on the latest executive instructions and amendments to the

Registration of Births and Deaths Act(amendment), 2023. These sessions were held via Zoom.

                       28
Joint District Births and Deaths Registrars, Statistical Assistants, and other CRS functionaries

were trained on the highlights of the amended Act, with presentations shared across districts.

           Tripura: State level training on CRS &MCCD were conducted time to time in State

Headquarter and District level/Block level training/orientation programmes were conducted

by the concerned authority.

           Uttarakhand: Training programmes related to birth and death registration were

held from time to time at Primary Health Centre, Community Health Centre, Chief Medical

Officers office in all the district of the state. Online training programme was also conducted by

the directorate of census operations for providing portal related information and online

registration to the registrar in the district. Trainees were trained on online registration and

issuance of certificate through CRS portal.

           Uttar Pradesh: During the financial year 2024-25, Three training sessions were

organized in all the districts for Newly appointed registrars. Training programmes were

organised at Sarojini Naidu Medical College in Agra and at J.N. Medical College in Aligarh

district.

           West Bengal: 49 training programmes were organized at District level during the

year 2024.

           Chandigarh: One training programme was conducted for Civil Registration

Functionaries during the year 2024.

           Delhi: Government of NCT of Delhi organized one day meeting cum training

programme on Civil Registration system of Delhi. Also, the Municipal corporation of Delhi

organized 12 Training programmes and Delhi Cantonment Board organized 1 training

programme for the Civil Registration functionaries.

           Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu: Training on the revamped CRS Portal

was provided to all Registrars and Data Entry Operators (DEOs) of Births and Deaths of DNH

& DD. In addition, hands-on training was provided to concerned registration functionaries

during the regular visits/inspections.

           Jammu & Kashmir: Two workshops were organized at the Union Territory level. In

addition, 51 training programmes were conducted at the district and block levels across the

UT of Jammu & Kashmir for civil registration functionaries during the year 2024.

           No Training: 6 States/UTs namely, Goa, Sikkim, Telangana, Andaman & Nicobar

Islands, Ladakh, and Puducherry, did not organize any training programme on Civil

Registration System during the year 2024. No information was received from three

                                             29
States/UTS namely, Assam, Maharashtra and Lakshadweep regarding Trainings conducted
during the year 2024.

2.9. Publicity efforts for improving Registration of Births and Deaths: Lack of

awareness about the need and importance of registration among the general public and lack

of procedural knowledge among the registration functionaries are among important reasons

for low levels of registration in the country. In order to make the public aware about the need

and importance of registration, some of the State Governments have undertaken various

publicity measures through various means with their own funds like printing of posters, cloth

banners, pamphlets, leaflets on the importance of registration of births and deaths. Radio talks

were also arranged on the importance of registration of births and deaths. In addition,

advertisements were published in leading newspapers. As per the details given by the States,

following publicity measures have been taken by them during the year: -

  Andhra Pradesh: Publicity on Civil Registration system has not been taken up in the

State due to lack of funds. However, the following steps were taken to create awareness on

registration of births and deaths during 2024: -

  · District Registrars were requested to instruct Registrars of Registration Units to

  ensure display of posters in all public places within the Registration Units.

  · Commissioner, Women & Child Department, was also requested to issue instructions

  to Project Directors to arrange for distribution and display of posters at Anganwadi

  Centers and to ensure Anganwadi workers notify Births / Deaths regularly to Village

  Secretaries.

  Arunachal Pradesh: Publicity measures were taken to create awareness among the

general public about the need, importance and benefits of births and deaths registration via

distribution of pamphlets through the students of various schools and public on 26th January

& 15th August 2024 and during the "Sarkar Apke Dwar Camps'' campaign through displaying

Banners/Posters in the stall of Economics & Statistics Department at General Ground, various

district during the period 2024.

  Bihar: The Information and Public Relations department of State Government

carried out publicity on the importance of registration of births and deaths by bringing out

advertisements in daily newspapers from time to time.

  Chhattisgarh: Publicity has been made through Poster pamphlets distribution to

public regarding procedure and importance of Birth and Death registration. Discussion in

                                  30
Gram Sabha regarding procedure and importance of Birth and Death registration. Besides this,

News were published in Newspaper.

  Goa: The production of birth certificate of a child is compulsory in the State for

admission to educational institutions as also for obtaining employment card, job recruitment,

civil marriage registration and for availing various State Government schemes such as Laadli

Laxmi scheme, Dayanand Social Security scheme, etc.

  Gujarat: Efforts are being made to create mass awareness about importance and

necessity of registration of births and deaths.

  Himachal Pradesh: Printed IEC material was distributed to all blocks in District

Solan during the year 2024.

  Karnataka: During the year 2024, wide publicity regarding importance of

registration of births & deaths were made through Newspapers and all publications published

by Directorate of Economics and Statistics. A full-fledged help desk with toll free number

1800-425-6578 has been established for providing information on birth and death

registration in e-JanMa software and to resolve the grievances for the public.

  Kerala: In creating awareness among the public regarding the importance and

necessity of registration of vital events, various publicity measures were taken in the year

2024 such as: -

  · Arranged publicity measures through Doordarshan and All India radio.

  · Permanent Notice Boards were exhibited in front of all registration units.

  · Social media like YouTube videos WhatsApp and departmental training programs are

  helping to popularize the importance of Civil Registration.

  · The details of services rendered, name of designated officer, time limit for registering

  births/deaths and the duties of the public under Civil Registration System are

  included in the `Citizen's Charter' published by the Gram Panchayats and

  Municipalities under the Kerala Panchayat Raj Act 1994 and Kerala Municipality Act

  1994.

  Madhya Pradesh: During the year 2024, advertisement in Newspaper were made to

increase awareness on Fake certificates.

  Maharashtra: Following publicity measures were adapted in the State: Instruction

to avoid fraudulent web sites while registering Birth & Death even, Official correspondence to

all Registrars for proper publicity In their jurisdiction to create awareness in general public ;

and an article regarding CRS published in `Arogya Patrika' of Information, Education &

Communication (IEC) Bureau of Public Health Department

                                          31
         Manipur: Public awareness through Radio Jingles was made during 2024.

         Mizoram: Adequate publicity has been made on the importance of Civil Registration.

Advertisements were often displayed in the daily newspapers and periodicals in local

language. Young Mizo Association (YMA), an NGO, and local council/village council took up

the responsibility of informing the public about the importance of reporting vital events to the

Registrar of births and deaths.

         Nagaland: The Department has been taking various steps for publicizing on the

importance of Registration of Births & Deaths, which were exhibited to the public in the form

of Posters, Pamphlets, Newspaper and TV advertisements with the messages of Births &

Deaths.

         Odisha: Adequate publicity through Electronic media, All India Radio, Display of

Posters, Distribution of leaflets, screening of the films etc., has been done in the State during

the year on registration of births and deaths to popularize the Civil Registration within

community.

         Punjab: The following publicity measures were taken by the State: -

  · The District Registrar and Local Registrars have been instructed to display boards or

wall painting on the various provisions of the Act and procedure at important places in their

offices for public awareness.

  · Instructions issued to display publicity material in the various Exhibitions organized

by the department at block /village level.

  · Regular announcements on the importance of registration of births and deaths have

been made through public address system of Village Gurudwaras. Paramedical staff also

spread awareness in immunization camps during their routine visits.

  · Instructions were issued to District and Local Registrars to use CRS logo and message

of birth and death registration in regular correspondence.

         Rajasthan: Publicity on registration of birth and death were made through Radio

Jingles, through appeal made on awareness on Births and Deaths Registration by District

administration and distribution of Pamplets among public during the year 2024.

         Tamil Nadu: The following publicity measures were taken by the State:

  · Various IEC (Information, Education, and Communication) modes were used to

create awareness including name in the Birth Certificates. Campaigns were conducted in

educational institutions and large public gatherings.

                                            32
  · Wide publicity was given to the extension of the time limit for name inclusion in birth

records until 31.12.2024. Advertisements were published in newspapers to inform the public

about the importance of registration and the extended time limit for name inclusion.

  · Sample IEC materials were shared with district authorities for display of IEC on name

registration in the delivery wards of Government medical institutions and public gathering

areas.

         Uttarakhand: Importance of Births & Deaths registration was discussed in

departmental and other meetings at district and block level and emphasis was laid on

informing the general public about the importance of birth and death registration. Special

emphasis was laid on 100% registration by creating public awareness through

ANM/Aaganwadi/ ASHA worker of the area. In addition, awareness among school children on

registration of birth and death was spread during other programs organized in schools.

         Uttar Pradesh: An amount of 2.44 crore was allocated to all districts by state to

publicize among general public through hoardings/wall paintings/FM radios at the district

levels.

         West Bengal: Normal publicity efforts were taken in some Districts during the year

2024.

         Andaman & Nicobar Islands: Intensive efforts were made by the Health department

for strengthening the effective internal mechanism of CRS in the Island during the year 2024.

         Chandigarh: Hoarding Boards have been installed in all government hospitals.

Dispensaries, all private hospitals and nursing homes and cremation grounds etc across

Chandigarh.

         Delhi: In the year 2024, during the field visits/surveys in the area/cluster pamphlets

containing the advantages of institutional deliveries related benefits under the Government

schemes, role of ASHA workers along with list of nearest hospitals were distributed among

the citizens by the O/o Chief Registrar (Births & Deaths). Awareness was provided through

pamphlets on how citizen can get birth certificate without any hassle in case of hospital

delivery. Publicity was done by MCD through website mcdonline.nic.in. Also, publicity efforts

for improving the registration of births and deaths were done by DCB.

         Jammu & Kashmir: Awareness and publicity programmes were conducted across

the UT of J&K to promote registration of births and deaths through the CRS Portal. Awareness

about Registration of Births and Deaths was generated during Public Darbars, Block Divas,

Health Melas, and special camps organized at district and block levels to address public

queries. Informational banners and pamphlets outlining the registration process were

             33
distributed through Block Development Officers to key stakeholders such as Chowkidars,

AWWs, ASHA Workers, ANMs, Dais, school headmasters, and caretakers of cremation/burial

grounds to improve registration coverage.

  Ladakh: The Department has publicized through different media platforms and

highlighted the importance of registration of births and deaths.

  Puducherry: IEC measures are being undertaken to publicize towards the coverage

of 100% registration.

  No Publicity: No publicity efforts have been made during the year 2024 in the

States/UTs of Haryana, Meghalaya, Sikkim, Telangana, and Tripura. No information is received

from Assam, Jharkhand, Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu and Lakshadweep.

2.10. Issuance of birth and death certificates: The Registrar under the provision of
Section 12 of the RBD Act, 1969 are required to give a Certificate from the register relating to
birth or death, free of charge to the person who gives the information under Section 8 and 9
of the said Act. In addition to that, under Section 17 of the RBD Act, the Certificate relating to
birth or death (any number of copies) has also been issued on the payment of prescribed fee
to the person asking for it. The information on Section 12 and 17 of the Act relating to issuance
of certificate is not available explicitly. However, under Section 12 of the RBD Act,
1969(Amended in 2023) all the Registrar are required to issue the certificate of registration
of births or deaths as soon as the registration of a birth or death has been completed but not
later than seven days. For the year 2024, Information received from 7 States /UTs namely Goa,
Gujarat, Karnataka, Mizoram, Punjab, Delhi and Puducherry under is presented in the
Statement 7.
