2.11. Difficulties encounter in implementation of the Act: Some of the States have
furnished the details of problems facing in implementing the provision of RBD Act and State

                                                                    34
Rules in the State/UT. The brief points given by the State Governments in their annual report
is summarized below: -

  Andhra Pradesh: No fresh sanction of posts of Statistical staff was made in 2024. The
lack of budget under CRS and MCCD towards trainings, Publicity, inspection and printing of
forms affects the successful implementation of RBD Act in the State. There are about 1.8 crore
legacy events in UBD Portal of State. The data porting from UBD to CRS software need to be
completed.

  Assam: Out of 35 districts, Only 18 have dedicated DPAs who has responsibility of
implementing the Act in their districts while other districts are managed by other category
officers.

  Bihar: Non-timely reporting of events by the informant and difficulties faced by the
Districts in implementation of the RBD Act were removed during the year 2024.

  Goa: All the Births & Deaths records from 1971 onwards have been computerized;
there is a possibility of damage to the physical Birth/Death records as the Registers containing
these records are worn out over the years. Therefore, this Office is planning to digitize these
physical records during the year 2024, so that the same can be stored in the digital format,
before getting fully damaged.

  Gujarat: Some post such as Research Officer, Research Assistant and Statistical
Assistant are lying Vacant this results in delay in administrative/technical work.

  Haryana: no publicity related activity was conducted during the year 2024 due to
lack of Budget.

  Himachal Pradesh: In State's hilly terrain, Poor internet connectivity and frequent
power outages and inadequate IT infrastructure at registration offices makes real time online
data entry challenging. Lack of training of Staff in new digital forms in amended Act(inclusion
of new fields etc). Moreover, shortage of manpower and budget in most of the health
institutions across State and District Headquarter is also affecting the routine working of
online CRS.

  Jharkhand: Some difficulties are encountered in implementing the provisions of RBD
Act and Rules in respect of delayed registration. Difficulties in timely submission of reports
and supervision due to lack of Block Statistical Supervisor at block level. Difficulties under
uniform CRS software in obtaining affidavit by general public for reporting events after 30
days of occurrence.

                                                                    35
  Karnataka: Inadequacy of staff at all levels. Lack of administrative authority of the
Chief Registrar over registration functionaries.

  Kerala: Pre-occupation of Registrars and other functionaries with other duties.
Damage caused to very old registration records. Non-registration of large chunk of infant and
child deaths particularly in rural areas. Inclusion of details without care in registration by the
public and request for correction of particulars in the registers as entered in other documents
pertaining to the individuals. Lack of proper training are some of the difficulties faced in the
implementation of the Act.

  Madhya Pradesh: Vacant posts of Assistant Statistical Officer, Statistical Clerks and
Clerks in various Municipal Corporations and Municipalities; Lack of control over Notifiers in
the rural areas.

  Maharashtra: In rural area yet to perceive, the real need of the registration of births
and deaths. The Gram Sevaks who is Ex-officio Registrar of births and deaths, has to cover
many villages due to vacant posts. The situation may improve with increase by appointing
Gram sevak where posts are vacant as well as if possible appointing assistant for day to day
birth and death registration work.

  Mizoram: The geographic location of the State has affected the registration process.
Gap in communication and transportation facilities hampers timely reports making it difficult
to consistently maintain 100% level of reporting.

  Nagaland: Inadequate postal, transportation and communication facilities. Lack of
remuneration for CRS functionaries, delay in submission of reports by Registrar and low level
of awareness and lack of Budget for CRS activities are some of major difficulties in the State.

  Odisha: There is shortage of staff/manpower in the State.
  Punjab: There is a shortage of Staff especially technical staff at all levels. More and
more technology is being involved in this work and various other softwares, being used by
different departments, are also being interlinked with the eSewa software.
  Rajasthan: Difficulties relating to implementation of Act in some cases such as
penalty, number of copies, adoption etc. were observed.
  Tamil Nadu: Non-availability of funds for sustaining the registration of births and
deaths through online facility in terms of software and hardware, inspection of registration
units, organizing training programme for CRS functionaries, Publicity on CRS at
State/Districts/Registration Unit levels etc.
  Telangana: Lack of Sub registrars and statistical staff in Municipalities. Large
number of vacant posts of Panchayats Secretaries.

                                                                    36
  Tripura: Revamped CRS ORGI portal was launched in the State on 5th June, 2024
and issues pertaining to problems faced by the State were informed through various modes.
Work in various Registration units get hampered and more technical support is need from the
Technical Team of ORGI.

  Uttarakhand: There is lack of registration information and the computerization of
old civil registration record/work is also not being done properly. Lack of manpower, training,
publicity, remote rural area and a lot of posts are lying vacant in birth-death registration cadre
of Health department are posing difficulties in effective implementation of RBD Act.

  West Bengal: As such, there is no difficulty in the implementation of the Act at the
field level. However, there are 196 registration units functioning without Data entry
operators. As per section 8(1)(a) of the RBD Act 1969 (Amendment 2023) the public has been
made responsible to give report of all births and deaths occurring in a house within the
stipulated time limit. As such extensive publicity measures are required to be adopted from
time to time for the awareness of the people for reporting of all vital events.

  Chandigarh: No major difficulties were encountered in the implementation of the Act
during the year 2024. All Government Registration Units, Private Hospitals, and Nursing
Homes are functioning smoothly on the CRS portal.

  Puducherry: No difficulties encountered in implementing RBD Act since compulsory
Registration of vital events is mandated under the RBD Act 1969 (Amendment 2023).

                                       

                                                                    37
                                                  CHAPTER ­ III

                             A BRIEF ANALYSIS OF DATA ON REGISTERED
                                 BIRTHS, DEATHS AND INFANT DEATHS

3.1 Births and deaths are the two most important vital events in the life of an individual
and for society as a whole. Based on the data collected under the Civil Registration System
on registered births and deaths, this chapter presents the trend in the number of registered
events and other related indicators, at the State and National level.
3.2 The Registration Unit in the Civil Registration System is the most important entity
responsible for registering events such as births and deaths, issuing of certificates,
maintenance of CRS records and reporting to higher authorities. The number of
registration units for the year 2024 at the national and States/UTs level with rural/urban
details may be seen in Statement 8.
3.3 The highest number of registration units i.e. 62780 are working in Uttar Pradesh,
followed by 44076 in Maharashtra, 38972 in Karnataka and 24550 in Madhya Pradesh.
Registration Units in 10 major States, namely, Uttar Pradesh, Maharashtra, Karnataka,
Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Tamil Nadu, Gujarat, Andhra Pradesh, Telangana and
Rajasthan accounts for about 88.5% of the total registration units of the country. In the case
of urban areas, the highest number of registration units are in Tamil Nadu (1984), followed
by 1822 in Uttar Pradesh.
3.4 At the national level, about 3.4 percent of Registration Units are located in Urban
areas and 96.6% of registration Units are in Rural areas.

2023, while in ten major states, namely Andhra Pradesh, Gujarat, Haryana, Jharkhand, Punjab,
Uttar Pradesh, Maharashtra, Tamil Nadu, and West Bengal birth registration has decreased in
the year 2024 compared to 2023.

3.9 Registration of Birth & Death within the prescribed time limit of 21 days
 3.9.1 Statement 13 shows the percentage of births and deaths registered within the prescribed
 time limit of 21 days. The registered births and deaths have been classified into four categories
 depending upon their period of registration. The four time periods considered for the purpose
 are: (i) within the prescribed time limit (up to 21 days), (ii) After 21 days but within 30 days,
 (iii) After 30 days but within 1 year, and (iv) More than 1 year. For 2024, Thirty-five (35)
 States/UTs have provided data on Births and Deaths Registration within the prescribed time
 limit (i.e. within 21 days).
 3.9.2 On the basis of data received from States/UTs the percentage of registration achieved
 out of the total registration undertaken in 2024 within the prescribed time limit (21 days) is as
 under:

than 80 to less than or equal to 90 percent, 13 States/UTs are in the category of more than 50
to less than or equal to 80 percent, and the remaining 7 States/UTs are under the category of
less than or equal to 50 percent.

3.9.5 Chart 5 shows the performance of major states in terms of the percentage of
registrations done within the prescribed time, i.e., 21 days. In seven states--namely Tamil
Nadu, Haryana, Punjab, Gujarat, Kerala, West Bengal, and Andhra Pradesh--the share of
registered births and deaths within 21 days is more than 80 % in terms of both total registered
births and deaths. In the case of Odisha, the share of timely registration is between 80 and 90
% in the case of births, and in the case of deaths, it is slightly lower, in the 70 to 80 % category.
While in the case of Maharashtra, Chhattisgarh, and Telangana, the share of timely registration
of births is between 70 and 80 % and the corresponding share of deaths is slightly higher, in
the range of 80 to 90 %. In the case of Karnataka and Assam, the share of within-time birth
registration is between 70 and 80 %, while in the case of deaths, it is slightly lower and is
between 60 and 70 %. In the case of Madhya Pradesh, Rajasthan, Uttar Pradesh, and Jharkhand,
the share of timely birth registration is between 60 and 70 %; for death registration, it is slightly
higher at 70 to 80 % for Madhya Pradesh and Rajasthan, and between 60 and 70 % for Uttar
Pradesh and Jharkhand. Finally, for the remaining two major states, Uttarakhand and Bihar,
timely registration of births is between 40 and 50 %, while the corresponding figures for deaths
range from 50 to 70 %.

3.14 Medical attention received at the time of birth and death

3.14.1 The complete information in respect of birth by type of medical attention at the time of
delivery and medical attention received by the deceased at the time of death during the year
2024 has been received from 35 States/UTs. As partial information was submitted by Sikkim,
its data has not been considered. In the case of Punjab, the relevant information excludes cases
registered more than 1 year of delay. The distribution of medical attention received at the time
of birth for these States/UTs has been illustrated in Chart 14. The data shows that the majority
of births have occurred in institutions, either in government or private ones. Institutional births
occurred in about 79.4% of cases, while 4.1% of the cases were attended by a
Physicians/Nurse/midwife. However, about 8.4% of the events were attended by an untrained
midwife/ Dai, and another 8.1% by others, including Not stated/Not Available.

3.14.2 In the case of Medical Attention received by deceased at the time of death, as evident
from Chart 15, 24.8% of the total registered deaths have occurred in institutions; about 11.9%
of registered deaths were reported under the category of medical attention other than
institutions, another 10.8% by others, including Not stated/Not Available and 52.5% of
deceased did not receive any medical attention at the time of death.

3.16 Still-Births

3.16.1 The World Health Organization (WHO) defines a still birth as a baby born with no signs
of life at or after 28 weeks' gestation. Still birth, also known as foetal death, has been defined as
`death prior to the complete expulsion or extraction from its mother of a product of human
conception, irrespective of the duration of pregnancy and which is not an induced termination of
pregnancy. The death is indicated by the fact that after such expulsion or extraction, the fetus does
not breathe or show any other evidence of life, such as beating of the heart, pulsation of the
umbilical cord, or definite movement of voluntary muscles. Heartbeats are to be distinguished
from transient cardiac contractions; stillbirths are to be distinguished from fleeting respiratory
efforts or gasps.'

3.16.2 Statement 22 presents stillbirths registered by place of occurrence (Rural /Urban) of
the event for the year 2024. It may be observed that in total registered stillbirths, the share of
rural registered stillbirths is only 31.0%, while this share is about 69.0.3% in Urban Areas.
State/UT-wise registered stillbirths is depicted in Table 4 (Page No. 79).

             Statement 22: Still-Births registered during 2024

Place of Occurrence  No. of Registered Still-Births   % Share of Registered Still-
                                                     Births by Place of Occurrence

      Rural                   25150                        31.0

      Urban                   55965                        69.0

      Total                   81117                        100.0

                                        57
Level of Registration (LOR)

3.17.1 The level of registration, defined as the percentage of registered births/deaths to the
births/deaths estimated through SRS, determines the performance of a State / Union Territory
in registration of births and deaths. In the absence of reliable estimates of number of births
and deaths on a year to year basis from any other source, the estimated number of events as
revealed from SRS is used to calculate the level of registration. The level of registration so
calculated is very high for some of the smaller States/Union Territories either due to the lower
estimates of vital events than the actual and or due to the fact that SRS estimates of vital events
are based on the place of usual residence while events are registered at the place of occurrence
irrespective of the place of residence.
3.17.2 Under this section, level of registration of Births & Deaths is based on revised mid-year
population projections based on census 2011. The levels of registration of births and deaths
for India and States/UTs for the period 2011 to 2019 and 2023-2024 are shown in Statements
23 and 24 respectively. The level of registration of births has increased from 86.6% in 2014 to
92.7 in 2019 and further rose to 99.1 in 2024 i.e. in last 11 years an increase of about 12.5%.
Similarly, the level of registration of deaths has also increased from 72.5% in 2014 to 92.0% in
2019 and 99.4% in 2024, showing an increase of 26.9% over the last 11 years. Chart 16 depicts
the trend in level of registration of births and deaths over the years. Due to continuous efforts
of the Central Govt., State Govts. and UT Admns. the gap between Levels of Birth and Death
Registration is narrowing down with a difference of 0.3% observed in 2024.

                      Chart 16: Level of Registration Births & Deaths over the years

                                                                                               99.1                                99.4

                              100.0   86.6                                     92.7      92.0
                               90.0                       72.5

3.17.9 Bihar, Gujarat, Haryana, Jharkhand, Karnataka, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Punjab,
Rajasthan, Tamil Nadu, Telangana, Uttarakhand & West Bengal are such major 11 States that

States Odisha, Andhra Pradesh, Assam, Chhattisgarh, Kerala and Uttar Pradesh were placed in
category 80-90% with respect to their level of birth registration. None of the Major States found
to be having level of registration of births lower than 80 percent. In case of level of registration
of deaths, 2 States namely Uttar Pradesh and Assam are in the category of 80 to 90 percent
while 2 States namely Bihar and Jharkhand are placed in the category of 50-80 percent level of
registration. No Major State is found to be having level of death registration below the 50
percent.

3.18 Sex Ratio at Birth

3.18.1 The Sex Ratio (Number of females per thousand males) at birth of registered events is an
important indicator to map the sex differential of the population at the beginning of their life.
The sex ratio at birth has been calculated after deducting the delayed registration of more than
1 year for the year 2024. Statement 26 shows Sex Ratio at Birth (SRB) based on registered events
for the years 2017-2024. Sex Ratio at Birth based on registered events for the year 2024 for All
India (excluding figures of Sikkim) stood at 933 females per thousand males.

3.20 Registered Births & Deaths of Indian Nationals in Indian Missions/Post abroad

3.20.1 Statement 29 presents the number of registered births and deaths during the year 2024
of Indian Nationals with Indian Mission/Post abroad. These events to Indian citizens occurring
abroad are registered by the respective Indian Consulates under the Citizenship Act, 1955. As
per the RBD Act Registrar General of India shall, subject to such rules as may be made by the
Central Government in this behalf, cause to be registered information as to births and deaths of
citizens of India outside India received by him under the rules relating to the registration of such
citizens at Indian Consulates made under the Citizenship Act, 1955 (57 of 1955), and every such
registration shall also be deemed to have been duly made under this Act.
